<C          At the request of the U.S. Army Research Institute, the National            0:::I
u        Research Council formed a committee to assess the field of techniques          <C
         that are claimed to enhance human performance. The Institute asked the         U
--
.....
"""
10
0
N
0
         Council to evaluate the claims made by proponents of selected existing
         techniques and to address two general additional questions: (I) What are
         the appropriate criteria for evaluating claims for such techniques in the
                                                                                        """
                                                                                        ~

0        future? (2) What research is needed to advance our understanding of            ~
N
a>       performance enhancement in areas related to the proposed techniques?           ~
(/)

         The objectives of the committee's study are to provide an authoritative        a>
                                                                                        (/)
a>       assessment of these questions for policymakers in research and devel-          cu

~        opment who are consumers of the techniques, as well as to consider their       2

                                                                                        lo..
LL         Many of the techniques under consideration grew out of the human             o
"C                                                                                      LL
a>       potential movement of the 1960s, including guided imagery, meditation,         "C
>        biofeedback, neurolinguistic programming, sleep learning, accelerated          ~
e
c.       learning, split-brain learning, and various techniques to reduce stress and    e
c.       increase concentration. Many of these techniques have gained popularity        §::
<C
         over the past two decades, promoted by persons eager to provide answers        <C
         to problems of human performance or to prosper from them. While often
         using the language of science to justify their approach, these promoters
         are for the most part not trained professionals in the social and behavioral
         sciences. Nonetheless, they do appeal to basic needs for human perform-
         ance, and the Army, like many other institutions, is attracted to the
         prospect of cost-effective procedures that can improve performance.

ailention to three general factors. First, the organizational (i.e., political,   defense of the questions probed and not probed, together with justification       <C

criteria, the types of evaluations considered feasible, and the extent to
which evaluation results will be used. Second, the program's conse-                                           Immediate Effects
quences should be described and explained, including planned and

                                THE NATURE OF THE PHENOMENA
               Parapsychologists divide psi-the term applied to all psychic phenom-
             ena-into two broad categories: extrasensory perception (ESP) and

                                                 169
 170                                 ENHANCING HUMAN PERFORMANCE                 PARANORMAL PHENOMENA                                                      171
    psychokinesis (PK). Included in ESP are telepathy, precognition, and         as the "hyperspatial nuclear howitzer," are claimed to have equally
    clairvoyance, all of which refer to methods of gathering information about   bizarre capabilities. Many of the sources cite the claim that Soviet
    objects or thoughts without the intervention of known sensory mecha-         psychotronic weapons were responsible for the 1976 outbreak of Legion-
    nisms. Popularly called mind over matter, PK refers to the influence of      naires' disease, as well as the 1963 sinking of the nuclear submarine

            Many of the techniques under consideration grew out of the human ~
 LL       potential movement of the 1960s, including guided imagery, meditation, "C
 "C
   a>     biofeedback, neurolinguistic programming, sleep learning, accelerated ~
   ec.>   learning, split-brain learning, and various techniques to reduce stress and
                                                                                         c.

          increase concentration. Many of these techniques have gained popularity c.
   c.     over the past two decades, promoted by persons eager to provide answers <C
  <C
          to problems of human performance or to prosper from them. While often
          using the language of science to justify their approach, these promoters
          are for the most part not trained professionals in the social and behavioral
          sciences. Nonetheless, they do appeal to basic needs for human perform-
          ance, and the Army, like many other institutions, is attracted to the
          prospect of cost-effective procedures that can improve performance.
